Visual witness description of PDF page 19 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A flowchart diagram showing the dependencies of choices at stage $k$. Six rounded rectangular boxes are arranged in two rows and three columns:
- Top-left: contains "$F_{j,k}$", "Approximate $y_1, \dots, y_k$", "in the current tuple". A blue arrow points right to the top-middle box.
- Top-middle: contains "$\varepsilon_k$", "Choose the tolerance", "from $H_k$ and $r_{k-1}$". A blue arrow points right to the top-right box, and a blue arrow points down to the bottom-middle box.
- Top-right: contains "$\alpha_k, w_k$", "Perturb the tuple;", "approximate", "the old $C^{(k-1)}$". A blue arrow points down to the bottom-right box.
- Bottom-right: contains "$G_{j,k}$", "Substitute the", "witness word:", "$F_{j,k}(X, X_{w_k})$". A blue arrow points left to the bottom-middle box.
- Bottom-middle: contains "$L_k, r_k$", "Choose a", "Lipschitz bound;", "fix the future budget". A blue arrow points left to the bottom-left box.
- Bottom-left: contains "Tail control", "$\sum_{\ell > k} \varepsilon_\ell \le r_k$", "Preserve earlier", "approximations".
```


<a id="eq:generator-tail"></a>
For $\ell>k$, the geometric decrease of the budgets gives $$\varepsilon_\ell<\frac{r_{\ell-1}}2
       \leq \frac{r_k}{2^{\ell-k}}.$$ Using <a href="#eq:stage-perturbation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stage-perturbation">[eq:stage-perturbation]</a>, we therefore obtain, for $p>k$, $$\max_j\left\lVert A_j^{(p)}-A_j^{(k)}\right\rVert
 \leq\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{p}\varepsilon_\ell
 \leq r_k.$$ Since $r_k\leq2^{-k}r_0$, each sequence $(A_j^{(k)})_k$ converges in operator norm to a unitary $A_j^{(\infty)}$, and $$\label{eq:generator-tail}
 \max_j\left\lVert A_j^{(\infty)}-A_j^{(k)}\right\rVert\leq r_k.$$ The limits are unitaries because the unitary group is norm closed. For every nonidentity reduced word $v\in\mathbb F_n$, $$\tau(A_v^{(\infty)})
   =\lim_{k\to\infty}\tau(A_v^{(k)})=0,$$ by operator-norm continuity of each fixed word evaluation and of the trace. Thus the limiting tuple has the free Haar word distribution.

It remains to prove that this tuple generates $M$. For every $j\leq k$, equations <a href="#eq:stage-new-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stage-new-approximation">[eq:stage-new-approximation]</a>, <a href="#eq:future-budget" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:future-budget">[eq:future-budget]</a>, and <a href="#eq:generator-tail" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:generator-tail">[eq:generator-tail]</a> yield <a id="eq:limit-target-approximation"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
\label{eq:limit-target-approximation}
 \left\lVert G_{j,k}(A^{(\infty)})-y_j\right\rVert_2
 &\leq
 \left\lVert G_{j,k}(A^{(\infty)})-G_{j,k}(A^{(k)})\right\rVert_2
  +\left\lVert G_{j,k}(A^{(k)})-y_j\right\rVert_2
 \notag\\
 &\leq L_k r_k+2\cdot2^{-k}
 \leq3\cdot2^{-k}.
\end{aligned}$$ Fixing $j$ and letting $k\to\infty$ through $k\geq j$ shows that $y_j$ belongs to the $L^2$ closure of the word algebra of $A^{(\infty)}$. Since the $y_j$ are dense, this word algebra is dense in all of $L^2(M,\tau)$.

To pass from this density to von Neumann generation, set $N=W^*(A_1^{(\infty)},\ldots,A_n^{(\infty)})$. The standard finite-trace conditional expectation $E_N:M\to N$ is trace-preserving and extends to the orthogonal projection of $L^2(M,\tau)$ onto $L^2(N,\tau)$. In particular it is $L^2$-contractive and fixes every element of $N$. Each $G_{j,k}(A^{(\infty)})$ belongs to $N$, so $$\left\lVert E_N(y_j)-y_j\right\rVert_2
 \leq 2\left\lVert G_{j,k}(A^{(\infty)})-y_j\right\rVert_2
 \longrightarrow0.$$ Hence the projection fixes the dense family $(y_j)$ and is the identity on $L^2(M,\tau)$. For $x\in M$, this implies $E_N(x)=x$ by faithfulness of $\tau$, and therefore $N=M$.

The limiting tuple is now a freely generating Haar $n$-tuple in $M$. Lemma <a href="#lem:haar-identification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:haar-identification">4</a> supplies a unital trace-preserving normal $*$-isomorphism from $L(\mathbb F_n)$ onto $M=L(\mathbb F_{n+1})$, proving the theorem. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-remark-1"></a>
<div class="remark">

*Remark 16*. Only the first $n$ generators are required to converge in operator norm. The completions $C^{(k)}$ may vary without a limit. The target approximations are in $L^2$, and the conditional expectation argument permits the operator norms of the approximating polynomials to grow with $k$.

</div>

<a id="sec:amplification"></a>
### Amplification to rank two

We recall the amplification convention for a $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $(Q,\tau_Q)$. Let $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}$ be the usual semifinite trace on $B(\ell^2)$, normalized to give every rank-one projection trace one. For $t>0$, choose a projection $p$ in the stabilized factor with $(\tau_Q\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}})(p)=t$ and set $$Q^t=p\bigl(Q\,\overline\otimes\,B(\ell^2)\bigr)p,
 \qquad
 \tau_{Q^t}=t^{-1}(\tau_Q\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}})\big|_{Q^t}.$$ Projections of every positive finite trace exist in this stabilized factor. Equivalence of projections of equal finite trace makes the normalized corner independent of the choice of $p$, up to a normal trace-preserving $*$-isomorphism.

Amplification respects normal tracial isomorphisms. Indeed, if $\Theta:Q\to R$ is such an isomorphism, the normal tensor-product map $\Theta\otimes\mathrm{id}$ preserves the semifinite trace and sends $p$ to a projection $p'$ of the same trace $t$. Its restriction is an onto normal isomorphism between the corresponding corners. It sends the corner unit $p$ to $p'$ and preserves their traces normalized by $t^{-1}$. We denote the resulting map by $\Theta^t:Q^t\to R^t$.

<a id="eq:interpolation-formulas"></a>
For real $s>1$, let $N_s=L(\mathbb F_s)$ be the interpolated free group factor, with the usual group factor at integer parameters. The established amplification formula is $$\label{eq:interpolation-formulas}
 N_s^t\cong N_{1+(s-1)t^{-2}}
 \qquad(s>1,\ t>0);$$ see (Dykema 1994, Theorem 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[dykema1994](#ref-dykema1994)]. This is an unconditional isomorphism formula for the interpolated family and does not assume that distinct parameters determine distinct isomorphism classes. It is the only interpolation theorem used in the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>.

<details id="proof-stmt-auto-remark-1"><summary>Proof of stmt-auto-remark-1</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a>.* Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:rank-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rank-step">1</a> with $n=3$ and $n=4$ to obtain $$N_3\cong N_4\cong N_5.$$ Let $\Theta:N_3\to N_5$ be the composition of these normal trace-preserving isomorphisms. Amplifying by $\sqrt2$ gives a unital normal trace-preserving isomorphism $$\Theta^{\sqrt2}:N_3^{\sqrt2}\longrightarrow N_5^{\sqrt2}.$$ Formula <a href="#eq:interpolation-formulas" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:interpolation-formulas">[eq:interpolation-formulas]</a> identifies its domain and range as follows: $$N_3^{\sqrt2}\cong N_{1+(3-1)/2}=N_2,
 \qquad
 N_5^{\sqrt2}\cong N_{1+(5-1)/2}=N_3.$$ These identifications and the amplified map compose to give $\Phi:L(\mathbb F_2)\to L(\mathbb F_3)$. Every map in the composition is a unital normal trace-preserving $*$-isomorphism. Thus $\Phi$ is bijective and has all the asserted properties. ◻

</details>

For completeness, we record the consequences for the whole interpolated family. Put $N_\infty=L(\mathbb F_\infty)$ and, for a $\mathrm{II}_1$ factor $Q$, define its fundamental group by $$\mathcal F(Q)=\{t>0:Q^t\cong Q\}.$$ Here and below the isomorphisms are normal and preserve the normalized traces. The classical free group factor alternative says that either all $N_r$, $1<r\leq\infty$, are isomorphic, or $N_r\not\cong N_s$ whenever $1<r<s\leq\infty$. The inclusion of the infinite parameter uses Rădulescu’s dichotomy (Rădulescu 1994, Corollary 4.7) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[radulescu1994](#ref-radulescu1994)], restated explicitly in (Goldbring and Pi 2025, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[goldbring_pi2025](#ref-goldbring_pi2025)]. Dykema’s corresponding dichotomy (Dykema 1994, Corollary 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[dykema1994](#ref-dykema1994)] is stated for finite parameters.

<a id="stmt-cor-all-interpolated"></a>
<div id="cor:all-interpolated" class="corollary">

**Corollary 17**. *For all $1<r,s\leq\infty$ there is a unital normal trace-preserving $*$-isomorphism $L(\mathbb F_r)\cong L(\mathbb F_s)$. Moreover, $$\mathcal F\bigl(L(\mathbb F_r)\bigr)=\mathbb R_{>0}
 \qquad(1<r\leq\infty).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-all-interpolated"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-all-interpolated</summary>

*Proof.* Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> rules out the pairwise-nonisomorphic alternative, so all parameters, including $\infty$, give the same isomorphism class. For finite $r>1$ and every $t>0$, formula <a href="#eq:interpolation-formulas" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:interpolation-formulas">[eq:interpolation-formulas]</a> then gives $$N_r^t\cong N_{1+(r-1)t^{-2}}\cong N_r.$$ Thus $\mathcal F(N_r)=\mathbb R_{>0}$. Finally, $N_\infty\cong N_2$ and functoriality of amplification give $N_\infty^t\cong N_2^t\cong N_2\cong N_\infty$ for every $t>0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:entropy-dimensions"></a>
### Free entropy dimensions and von Neumann generators

For a bounded self-adjoint tuple $Z$ in a tracial von Neumann algebra, let $\delta(Z)$ and $\delta_0(Z)$ denote Voiculescu’s microstates free entropy dimensions. The second is the modified version using entropy in the presence of the perturbing semicircular family. Write $\delta^*(Z)$ and $\delta^\star(Z)$ for the nonmicrostates dimensions defined through nonmicrostates free entropy $\chi^*$ and free Fisher information $\Phi^*$, respectively. We use the conventions in (Voiculescu 2002b, sec. 2.4) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[voiculescu2002entropy](#ref-voiculescu2002entropy)] and (Connes and Shlyakhtenko 2005, sec. 4.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[connes_shlyakhtenko2005](#ref-connes_shlyakhtenko2005)].

By their definitions, all four quantities depend only on the joint tracial $*$-distribution of the tuple. This is weaker than invariance under a change of von Neumann generators: two tuples can generate the same von Neumann algebra without having the same joint distribution. The isomorphisms proved above show that the four dimensions do change under such a replacement.

<a id="stmt-cor-entropy-dimensions"></a>
<div id="cor:entropy-dimensions" class="corollary">

**Corollary 18** (Dependence on von Neumann generators). *Let $M=L(\mathbb F_2)$ with its canonical trace. For every integer $n\geq2$ there are a self-adjoint $n$-tuple $X^{(n)}$ and a self-adjoint $2n$-tuple $Y^{(n)}$ such that $$W^*(X^{(n)})=W^*(Y^{(n)})=M$$ and $$\delta(X^{(n)})=\delta_0(X^{(n)})=n,
 \qquad
 \delta^*(Y^{(n)})=\delta^\star(Y^{(n)})=n.$$ Consequently, none of $\delta,\delta_0,\delta^*,\delta^\star$ is invariant under changing a finite self-adjoint $W^*$-generating tuple of a tracial von Neumann algebra. Each takes unbounded values on such generating tuples of the single factor $L(\mathbb F_2)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-entropy-dimensions"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-entropy-dimensions</summary>

*Proof.* Fix an integer $n\geq2$. Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">2</a> and successive applications of Theorem <a href="#thm:rank-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rank-step">1</a> provide a unital normal trace-preserving $*$-isomorphism $$\theta_n:L(\mathbb F_n)\longrightarrow M;$$ for $n=2$ we may take the identity. If $Z$ is any finite self-adjoint tuple in $L(\mathbb F_n)$ and $p$ is a noncommutative $*$-polynomial, then $$\tau_M\bigl(p(\theta_n(Z))\bigr)=\tau_{L(\mathbb F_n)}\bigl(p(Z)\bigr).$$ Thus coordinatewise transport by $\theta_n$ preserves the joint tracial $*$-distribution and hence each of the four dimensions. Normality and surjectivity also give $W^*(\theta_n(Z))=\theta_n(W^*(Z))$.

Let $S^{(n)}=(s_1,\ldots,s_n)$ be a freely independent variance-one semicircular tuple in the semicircular realization of $L(\mathbb F_n)$ (Voiculescu 1990, Remarks 1.10–1.11) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[voiculescu1990](#ref-voiculescu1990)]. It generates $L(\mathbb F_n)$ as a von Neumann algebra, and the standard semicircular computations give $$\delta(S^{(n)})=\delta_0(S^{(n)})=n;$$ see (Voiculescu 2002b, sec. 2.4(c) and (d)) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[voiculescu2002entropy](#ref-voiculescu2002entropy)] for $\delta$ and (Brannan et al. 2023, sec. 5, opening paragraph) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[brannan_elzinga_harris_yamashita2023](#ref-brannan_elzinga_harris_yamashita2023)] for $\delta_0$. Set $X^{(n)}=\theta_n(S^{(n)})$. The preceding transport observations prove the required generation and the two microstates values.

For the nonmicrostates dimensions, let $u_1,\ldots,u_n$ be the canonical group unitaries in $L(\mathbb F_n)$ and put $$a_j=\frac{u_j+u_j^*}{2},\qquad
 b_j=\frac{u_j-u_j^*}{2\mathrm i},\qquad
 G^{(n)}=(a_1,b_1,\ldots,a_n,b_n).$$ The entries are self-adjoint, and $u_j=a_j+\mathrm ib_j$ and $u_j^*=a_j-\mathrm ib_j$. Hence they generate the algebraic group algebra $\mathbb C\mathbb F_n$ as a unital $*$-algebra, and generate $L(\mathbb F_n)$ as a von Neumann algebra. Apply Mineyev and Shlyakhtenko’s group-algebra theorem (Mineyev and Shlyakhtenko 2005, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[mineyev_shlyakhtenko2005](#ref-mineyev_shlyakhtenko2005)] to this tuple, before transporting it. It gives $$\delta^*(G^{(n)})=\delta^\star(G^{(n)})
 =\beta_1^{(2)}(\mathbb F_n)-\beta_0^{(2)}(\mathbb F_n)+1=n.$$ Here $\beta_0^{(2)}(\mathbb F_n)=0$ and $\beta_1^{(2)}(\mathbb F_n)=n-1$ are the usual $L^2$-Betti numbers of the free group; see (Connes and Shlyakhtenko 2005, sec. 2.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[connes_shlyakhtenko2005](#ref-connes_shlyakhtenko2005)]. Define $Y^{(n)}=\theta_n(G^{(n)})$. Again its joint tracial $*$-distribution is unchanged and it generates $M$, proving the remaining assertions. Taking $n=2$ and $n=3$ gives different values on generators of the same factor, and letting $n$ grow gives unboundedness. ◻

</details>

The nonmicrostates computation uses algebraic generators of $\mathbb C\mathbb F_n$ before transport. The transported tuples all generate $L(\mathbb F_2)$ as a von Neumann algebra, but their algebraic $*$-algebras are not held fixed as $n$ varies. Thus the corollary concerns $W^*$-generation and is compatible with the algebraic-generator invariance in (Mineyev and Shlyakhtenko 2005, Theorem 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[mineyev_shlyakhtenko2005](#ref-mineyev_shlyakhtenko2005)].

      "caption": "The choices at stage $k$ follow the arrows. The witness word $w_{k}$ is found only after $ε_{k}$ is fixed.       "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n  stage/.style={draw=blue!45!black, rounded corners=2pt,\n    fill=blue!3, text width=3.65cm, minimum height=2.1cm,\n    align=center, inner sep=7pt, font=\\small},\n  choice/.style={-{Stealth[length=2.3mm]}, thick, draw=blue!45!black},\n  node distance=9mm and 9mm\n]\n\\node[stage] (old) {$F_{j,k}$\\\\[3pt]\n  Approximate $y_1,\\ldots,y_k$\\\\in the current tuple};\n\\node[stage, right=of old] (epsilon) {$\\varepsilon_k$\\\\[3pt]\n  Choose the tolerance\\\\from $H_k$ and $r_{k-1}$};\n\\node[stage, right=of epsilon] (word) {$\\alpha_k,\\ w_k$\\\\[3pt]\n  Perturb the tuple;\\\\approximate the old $C^{(k-1)}$};\n\\node[stage, below=of word] (new) {$G_{j,k}$\\\\[3pt]\n  Substitute the witness word:\\\\\n  $F_{j,k}(X,X_{w_k})$};\n\\node[stage, below=of epsilon] (budget) {$L_k,\\ r_k$\\\\[3pt]\n  Choose a\\\\Lipschitz bound;\\\\fix the future budget};\n\\node[stage, below=of old] (tail) {Tail control\\\\[3pt]\n  $\\sum_{\\ell>k}\\varepsilon_\\ell\\leq r_k$\\\\\n  Preserve earlier approximations};\n\\draw[choice] (old) -- (epsilon);\n\\draw[choice] (epsilon) -- (word);\n\\draw[choice] (word) -- (new);\n\\draw[choice] (new) -- (budget);\n\\draw[choice] (budget) -- (tail);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A flowchart diagram showing the dependencies of choices at stage $k$. Six rounded rectangular boxes are arranged in two rows and three columns:\n- Top-left: contains \"$F_{j,k}$\", \"Approximate $y_1, \\dots, y_k$\", \"in the current tuple\". A blue arrow points right to the top-middle box.\n- Top-middle: contains \"$\\varepsilon_k$\", \"Choose the tolerance\", \"from $H_k$ and $r_{k-1}$\". A blue arrow points right to the top-right box, and a blue arrow points down to the bottom-middle box.\n- Top-right: contains \"$\\alpha_k, w_k$\", \"Perturb the tuple;\", \"approximate\", \"the old $C^{(k-1)}$\". A blue arrow points down to the bottom-right box.\n- Bottom-right: contains \"$G_{j,k}$\", \"Substitute the\", \"witness word:\", \"$F_{j,k}(X, X_{w_k})$\". A blue arrow points left to the bottom-middle box.\n- Bottom-middle: contains \"$L_k, r_k$\", \"Choose a\", \"Lipschitz bound;\", \"fix the future budget\". A blue arrow points left to the bottom-left box.\n- Bottom-left: contains \"Tail control\", \"$\\sum_{\\ell > k} \\varepsilon_\\ell \\le r_k$\", \"Preserve earlier\", \"approximations\"."
